9 Classics Where the Audiobook Version Is Better

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Audiobooks were technically born as far back as 1877, with Thomas Edison's invention of the phonograph. However, it wasn't until 1932 that the American Foundation for the Blind officially named them "Talking Books," introducing them as a medium for visually impaired people to enjoy literature as an alternative to Braille. Throughout the entire 20th century, and even well into the 21st, many classics have been turned into audiobooks so phenomenal that they're arguably even better than the original book.

Audiobooks and physical books are, of course, vastly different media that fulfill vastly different purposes, so comparing them can feel a bit like comparing apples to oranges. Still, sometimes, an audiobook is so good that it becomes entirely reasonable to say it provides a better experience than the original. These are audiobooks that anyone who enjoys classic literature should consider essential listening material. "Classic" is defined for the purposes of this list as any book that was originally published before the 21st century.

Folkloric vampires predate Bram Stoker's Dracula by hundreds of years. Inspired by such stories, as well as by Irish folklore and historical figures like Vlad the Impaler, Stoker wrote the 1897 classic that is nowadays widely remembered as the most foundational pillar of modern vampiric fiction. It's undoubtedly one of the best horror books of all time, which made the announcement of an original Audible audiobook-slash-audio-drama all the more worthy of skepticism.

In the end, however, Bram Stoker's Dracula ended up being one of the greatest horror audiobooks that readers have been treated to in years. With a stacked cast that includes the likes of Ella Purnell, Jonathan Bailey, and Aimee Lou Wood, the audiobook takes the classic epistolary novel and makes it feel even more immersive, cinematic, and well-paced. It's worth noting that it's an abridged audio drama rather than a full reading of the original novel, which is not ideal, but those interested in such a concept should love it.

Originally serialized between 1875 and 1877, Russian author Leo Tolstoy's Anna Karenina is widely regarded as one of the most foundational pieces of world literature in history, a must-read masterpiece whose daunting 1,000-page length shouldn't deter anyone from considering it essential reading. Since it was one of her favorite books of all time, Maggie Gyllenhaal agreed to narrate an Audible original audiobook that really does justice to one of the best epic books of all time.

Tolstoy's novel was such a monumental artistic achievement that the author was notoriously drained and exhausted by the whole experience by the time he was done, and reading such a vast book can itself also be a massive undertaking. With the raw immediacy and many emotional variations of Gyllenhaal's narration, listening to Anna Karenina can be a far more engaging and far less tedious experience than many may initially think. At over 30 hours long, it's definitely a time commitment, but oh so worth every minute.

Upon publication back in 1997, J. K. Rowling's Harry Potter and the Philosopher's Stone (published in the US as Harry Potter and the Sorcerer's Stone) was an immediate commercial and critical hit, kicking off one of the biggest multimedia franchises in history. The book's first audiobook version came out in 1999, and the performers varied by region. Whereas Jim Dale narrated the book in the US and Canada, it was Stephen Fry's voice that was heard in the British and international releases. If there is any one Harry Potter book that's better heard than read, it's Fry's performance of Philosopher's Stone.

As a whole, this is one of the most essential fantasy books to read before kicking the bucket, but there's nothing quite like hearing it through Fry's rich, velvety baritone voice. On the page, Philosopher's Stone is beautifully imaginative and cozy, but at times feels like an overly simple debut novel in terms of Rowling's prose. Fry elevates every word and makes the entire story feel like a wonderful fairy tale being told by the fire on a cold weekend night outdoors.

One of Stephen King's first novels was 1983's Pet Sematary, published before he had fully established and cemented himself as the true King of Horror. Fans had been begging for an audiobook for years, and once the project finally materialized, it was Michael C. Hall of Dexter fame who publisher Simon & Schuster picked as the right voice to narrate the story. The result is one of the best Stephen King audiobooks to date, and considering just how many great ones there are, that's saying a lot.

Pet Sematary was already absolutely legendary well before 2018, widely regarded by many—including King himself—as the author's most genuinely terrifying work. However, once the horror aspects really pick up steam after a relatively slow-burning first few pages, Hall makes it hit even harder. Getting an actor well-known for projects as death-related as Six Feet Under and Dexter to narrate a horror novel all about death and grief was not only brilliant meta-casting, but also hugely beneficial for the story as a whole.

Multiple books are almost always brought up when the endless debate of what "The Great American Novel" is gets brought up, but John Steinbeck's East of Eden is the most noteworthy. This family saga was likely key to Steinbeck being awarded the Nobel Prize in Literature in 1962, and it more than lives up to its reputation as one of the most perfect American books ever written. The first audiobook version of the classic ever recorded was narrated by respected character actor Richard Poe.

East of Eden was already widely recognized as one of the most perfectly written masterpieces of all time, and it's definitely a must-read, but there's something about the way Poe complements the narrative that makes his version arguably even more unforgettable. Steinbeck wrote East of Eden in such a sweeping, almost Biblical voice that it almost feels like the narration of a legend, and Poe's resonant yet deeply human voice allows that narration style to feel all the more intimate and personal.

The book that earned Harper Lee a Pulitzer Prize was 1960's To Kill a Mockingbird, one of the most essential and widely discussed American classics of the 20th century. Though certain elements of its narrative are undeniably dated by modern standards, its heart will forever be in the right place, with its critiques of racial prejudice and systemic injustice being as timely and hard-hitting today as they were back in the '60s. In 2014, legendary actress Sissy Spacek was tasked with narrating the audiobook version of the story.

The reasons why this is one of those great books that are even better as audiobooks are aplenty, and even though the Pulitzer-winning original remains absolutely foundational, Spacek brings a level of nuance and authenticity to the book that feels like a much-welcome sense of modernity. The actress' beautifully melodic Texan drawl makes the small-town Alabama setting feel all the more evocative and atmospheric, and the way she flawlessly captures Scout's personality as both a child and an adult is incredible.

Stephen King's 22nd book was 1986's It, still remembered by many as the author's best and most iconic work to date. At over 1,100 pages in length, it's an absolute brick of a horror novel, but its epic scope still works marvelously all these many years later. To narrate the 2016 audiobook, Simon & Schuster decided to go for an actor who already had a pre-existing tie to the Stephen King universe: Steven Weber, who played Jack Torrance in the 1997 three-part miniseries version of The Shining.

It was already one of those horror books where every chapter is perfect—something quite admirable for such a thick novel—but Weber took that literary doorstop and somehow turned it into an even bigger, more immersive 45-hour juggernaut of an audiobook. The actor gives each of the Losers a distinct voice both in their child and adult versions, and he goes all in on Pennywise without ever teetering too far into distracting camp. Perfectly paced and intense to the core, It is a must-listen-to audiobook through and through.

American poet, essayist, and civil rights activist Maya Angelou was a towering figure in modern American literature, completely reshaping how Black American identity was expressed in the written word. The first of her seven published memoirs, 1969's I Know Why the Caged Bird Sings, broke new ground and became one of the first major autobiographies by an African American woman to achieve global bestseller status. When an audiobook version was released in 2011, it was Angelou herself who narrated it.

Taking one of the best memoirs of all time and turning it into an audiobook worthy of its legacy was always going to be a tough task, so who better to achieve it than the author herself? By having Angelou narrate her own story with her unmistakably theatrical contralto voice, I Know Why the Caged Bird Sings feels even more honest and intimate than it already does on the page, balancing poignancy and humor to absolute perfection.

Well before he wrote what many still consider the greatest and most influential fantasy epic of the 20th century, J. R. R. Tolkien published the fantasy children's classic The Hobbit, almost inarguably the most perfect fantasy novel of the 1930s. There are multiple audiobook versions of The Hobbit available, but one reigns supreme as perhaps the audiobook that most elevates the experience of the original text: the 2020 version narrated by Andy Serkis.

It's easily one of the best audiobooks of all time, with Serkis not only delivering a much-expected powerhouse performance as Gollum, but also giving each of the other characters a distinct voice that makes them even more memorable than they are on paper. The Hobbit is also filled with songs and poems, and Serkis brings them to life in a way that reading the physical book simply can't match. In Serkis' voice, The Hobbit really does feel like the wonderful myth that it was always meant to be.

Brian Flores: Vikings plan to rotate players in order to deal with altitude in Mexico City

📅 2026-11-17  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The 49ers are spending the week in Colorado in order to acclimate to the altitude at which their Week 11 game in Mexico City will be played. The Vikings are not.

But the Vikings have a plan for ensuring that their players are ready for the conditions.

“It’s a tough place to play,” Vikings defensive coordinator Brian Flores told reporters on Tuesday regarding the game in Mexico. “I think it’s certainly taxing, you know, I think you’re — I think as a staff, we’re already talking about, ‘Hey, we’re gonna have to rotate these guys and get, you know, everybody’s gonna have to be ready to play.’ And so, yeah, I mean, it’s certainly something that we’re going to have to deal with.

“But there’s always, you know, elements that we’re dealing with, whether it’s cold or rain or things like that. Obviously, we’re indoors, so we don’t deal with much of that. But that’s the environment we’re gonna be in. I think we prepare them to play in that environment, but I’m not gonna be silly and say, ‘Hey, everybody, hey, you’ve got to go in there for 12 straight plays.’ I mean, we’re not doing that.

“So we’ll have rotations, we’ll have a plan in place. Inevitably, we’re gonna have to fight through some of that. It’s not gonna be your average game where, you know, altitude, you just have — you literally have less oxygen. So we’ve got to do a great job as a staff of making sure — watching guys, and making sure we got guys in and out, you know, as much as we can.”

His broader message to the defense is clear: “We’re gonna need everybody.”

Regardless of the plan to rotate players, the question becomes whether the 49ers will be able to rely more heavily on their best players because they’re properly acclimated to the thinner air. The Vikings are choosing a different strategy, and it’s going to create an obvious apples to apples comparison between the approaches that the two teams have taken — especially if the 49ers players seem to be fresh and the Vikings players seem to be gassed.

Gael Cameron Says Her ‘Below Deck’ Romance With Nathan Gallagher Was Abusive: ‘I Am Still Scared’

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Almost a year after Below Deck star Nathan Gallagher was arrested for domestic abuse in Australia — and 12 weeks after the news went public — Gallagher’s former partner and cast member Gael Cameron is finally speaking out about their relationship.

Cameron broke her silence on an episode of the podcast We Need To Talk, hosted by Paul C. Brunson, where she publicly accused Gallagher of abuse for the first time and described a toxic relationship that’s left her in fear to this day.

“I know that he is not afraid to do anything to throw me under. He’s not afraid to lie… He’s always had this idea that he has ownership or control over me,” Cameron said tearfully. “I still feel that. And I am still scared of him. And that’s why I’ve not said anything, even though he’s thrown all these accusations out there.”

Gallagher and Cameron met while filming Season Nine of Below Deck: Mediterranean in 2023, both working on the superyacht’s deck team. Over three seasons, their relationship evolved from flirty co-workers to welcoming a son together last June. But this August — halfway through a season where Gallagher struggled to juggle his bosun duties and be a present father — news broke of Gallagher’s December 2025 arrest on domestic violence charges. According to Australian police, Gallagher attacked Cameron at her apartment in Sydney, resulting in his arrest on Dec. 27, 2025. During the physical altercation, Gallagher “placed both of his hands around the victim’s neck applying enough pressure which made her unable to breathe,” according to police records. He was charged with assault occasioning actual bodily harm, common assault, and intentionally choking a person without consent.

Although Gallagher previously told Rolling Stone he had proof that would clear his name, he failed to appear at a mandated court hearing in September. As a result, he was convicted by default on charges of assault. Australian authorities issued a warrant for his arrest, noting Gallagher was suspected to have fled the country. Bravo has since announced it has no plans to work with him.  Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

Both Bravo and Gallagher declined to comment further to Rolling Stone.

During the podcast episode, Cameron charts the timeline of her relationship with Gallagher, including the behind-the-scenes struggles that kept her silent and afraid. Cameron first met Gallagher while filming Season Nine of Below Deck: Mediterranean in 2023. At the time, she was dating another man but began a friendship with Gallagher. Their working relationship was often “playfully flirty” when the cameras were rolling, but they didn’t start seeing each other until filming wrapped.

In 2023, the two were in a long-distance relationship, but Cameron said there were early signs of anger issues in Gallagher. When they were able to meet up, Cameron described his “love bombing,” showering her with gifts and affection. When she found out she was pregnant, Gallagher convinced her to keep the baby — the first of many moves which she claims he did in order to isolate her.

“If I ever said, ‘I don’t know if I can do this. I don’t think it’s the right time,’ He would make me feel very bad about myself. He would make me feel very guilty and just terrible about myself,” she said. “I have quite a conservative mum. I couldn’t talk to anyone. So he was kind of my only sounding board because I was so afraid to tell anyone.”

But the pregnancy only aggravated Gallagher’s behavior, according to Cameron. She said that she spent close to “90 percent” of her pregnancy in tears, often feeling like any small mistake would trigger Gallagher’s anger. In one instance, she describes that while drunk, Gallagher jumped out of a moving car rather than talk to her. Once a social worker told her the relationship was “unsafe,” Cameron began reevaluating if they could stay together long term. “I’d be told to shut the fuck up. I’d be called a bitch, a slut, and all these horrible things,” she says. “There were multiple times when I was like ‘No no no, this cannot continue,’ because my son cannot grow up thinking that this is how you speak to women.” But then Below Deck came to call.  Related Content Hayden Panettiere's Mother Blames Brian Hickerson For Star’s Death Kid Rock's Nashville Bar Sued After Bouncers Allegedly Beat Patron Who 'Tapped' Guitar Lawsuit Accusing Selena Gomez and Wondermind Co-Founders of Fraud Is Dismissed Priscilla Presley Scores Partial Win Over Fraud Suit, But Claims About Lisa Marie’s Death Can Stand

When Gallagher flew to Croatia to film Below Deck: Mediterranean Season 11, Cameron didn’t initially want to go. She appeared on the series as a guest star, and lived off of the boat with her infant son. But she believes Gallagher pushed her to come to Croatia as another form of control. “It felt as though he was very worried about who I spoke to or who I was around when he wasn’t around,” she says. “There was this element of control that he would lose over me if I wasn’t in the same country as him.” She also describes incidents during heated fights, when Gallagher would record on his phone, pretending that she was striking him or hitting him unprovoked. Things came to a head on Dec. 27, 2025, when Cameron said Gallagher choked her while their son was in her arms, which led to Gallagher’s arrest and subsequent conviction. Cameron appeared visibly emotional when she recounted the night.

“When I was in the bathroom, and he had his hands around my neck and attempted to lift, my son was in my arms. All I could think is, ‘If I fall, so does he.’ From that moment, I should have known there’s no return,” she says. “When he had me pinned down and he was striking me on the jaw, I actually sat there and I asked if he was going to kill me. There was a moment where I actually asked that question. The reason I say this is because if things had not gotten that bad, I would never have gotten out. According to Cameron, she has not spoken to Gallagher or been in contact since he fled the country. But she says that his departure has changed her life for the better.

“Him leaving was the biggest weight off my shoulders. All of a sudden, I could have my family over to help me look after my son when I went to work. I could breathe again,” she says. “I had been living this hell. So life felt so bright.”

Google commemorates Robert Pattinsons James Bond casting with a sneaky Easter egg

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Robert Pattinson's casting as James Bond has left Google feeling shaken, not stirred.

Following the announcement that Pattinson would be our next 007, Google introduced a new Bond-themed Easter egg. If you search up Pattinson's name, the search results shake a little, and a small twist of lemon falls from the top of the screen to rest daintily on Pattinson's head.

The Easter egg is a nod to James Bond's classic cocktail order: a vodka martini, shaken, not stirred. Google has essentially turned Pattinson into the cocktail he'll be ordering onscreen.

Pattinson's casting as Bond came after weeks of speculation and reported screen tests for potential Bond candidates, including Callum Turner, Jack Lowden, Josh O'Connor, and Nicholas Hoult.

Pattinson will star in the as-yet unnamed 26th Bond film, the first since Daniel Craig took his last bow in 2021's No Time To Die. Dune helmer Denis Villeneuve is set to direct. The pair have also worked together on the upcoming Dune: Part Three. Villeneuve and Pattinson's Bond film does not yet have a release date.

Bond won't be the first franchise Pattinson has led to be commemorated with a Google Easter egg. In 2022, tied to The Batman's release, if you searched terms such as "Bruce Wayne" or "Gotham City," a yellow Bat-Signal would appear alongside the results. Clicking on it turned the page into a moody Gotham night, complete with thunder, the Bat-Signal, and Batman swinging around the screen.

Plus, if you search up "Twilight" now, you'll be treated to a small line of scrolling text that reads "hoa hoa hoa season," flanked by falling leaf emojis. The Easter egg references Twilight fans' loving nickname for fall, drawn from the so-called "hoa hoa hoa" vocals of Blue Foundation's "Eyes on Fire" from the Twilight soundtrack. 11 Climber Gift Ideas You Probably Haven’t Thought of Yet, According to Editors

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

(Photo: L to R: Anthony Walsh, Etsy Glass Carabiner Shop, Metolius, Yosemite Climbing Association, Kong)

If you’ve already taken a lap through our gift guide and still need more ideas, we’ve got you. All three of us editors here at Climbing have selected our top picks for gifts this year, from gear we’ve got our eyes on, to accessories in the climbing zeitgeist right now.

Some of these editor gifts picks make perfect mini gifts for stocking stuffers or for that climber partner who you want to thank for giving you the perfect soft catch this past year. Other picks make a bigger impact, from a super lightweight harness to a great gym bag. Most of our picks are great for a variety of climbers, whether it’s your new boyfriend who you hope to convert into a climber, or your mentor who’s been at it for eons.

We hope these gift ideas make your holiday shopping as painless as a splitter hand crack so you can spend more time training or climbing, and less time scrolling through endless product pages and catalogs.

Originally designed and hand-sewn by Nate Barton—the Missoula-based climber who ran the meme account @beta.sprayer until 2024—the Lolo Hoodie V1 ($169.99) is a functional midlayer for cold-weather climbing and the flagship product of the fledgling brand Lolo Hardware. Its large, zippered chest pocket was originally intended to help ice climbers warm up their hands between pitches.

Having pretty much lived in this hoodie for the past three winters, I’ve found the chest pocket far superior to any sort of pant pocket for storing my phone, gloves, and snacks. Plus, with my items against my chest, I never have to worry about my phone freezing or my car keys accidentally slipping out during a chimney. From Joshua Tree in January to Canyonlands in November, this is a comfy and durable hoodie for the coldest days and nights of climbing.

It’s 2026, folks: If you’re still using mined chalk instead of seawater-sourced chalk, this is your call to finally make the switch. Climbing’s feature on the hidden environmental impact of chalk showed how the vast majority of magnesium carbonate comes from mines in China, where magnesite factories poison the water and soil of nearby residents.

Luckily, there are plenty of sustainable brands that produce seawater-sourced chalk by using solar power to evaporate ocean brine from seawater. Here are some of our favorites:

Shop Midnight Lightning Sea Grip Liquid Chalk

For climbers with pierced ears, a pair of carabiner earrings is a customizable accessory that never gets old. Recently, I’ve been getting tons of compliments on my pair of silver, locker draw earrings (yes, they actually screw and unscrew!) that I bought at the Yosemite Climbing Museum in July. Those are only available at the museum, so if you’re not currently near Mariposa, California, here are a few other great places to buy carabiner earrings online:

After breaking a cross-loaded carabiner in a scary trad fall, I now always add some sort of tape or rubber band to keep cam carabiners in place. I wrote about my DIY solution a couple of years ago, but now I simply slide on Avant Climbing’s Flip-Stop Cam Carabiner Stabilizers ($22.50 for a 10-pack). The Flip-Stop makes for easy cam clipping when mega pumped. But unlike my rubber band hack, it also keeps the cam sling open for clipping a long draw into. Crucially, like a quickdraw’s dogbone, the Flip-Stop prevents carabiners from rotating onto the dangerously weak X-axis. Nothing ruins the holidays like a pair of broken ankles.

The new Arc’teryx Alpha SL Harness ($180) may weigh less than the gift wrap paper you’ll hide it in. At an absurdly light 78 grams (2.7oz), the Alpha SL is for those going ultralight in the mountains, whether that’s an alpine rock romp or a big ski traverse. This harness is also surprisingly comfortable to rappel in, thanks to its uber-stiff Aluula-composite construction. And it fits a range of body types thanks to its adjustable leg loops and five waistbelt sizes. Just don’t set it down unattended on a glacier—it’ll blow away in the slightest gust of wind.

The Patagonia Airshed Pro Pullover ($169) might have been built for mountain running, but I wear mine year-round on all sorts of objectives, from sunny rock climbs to cold north faces. The Airshed Pro is a pseudo-windlayer: decently breathable for high-output activities, yet stills blocks the wind and has a water-repellent coating. The result is a shirt equally at home skiing on a glacier or rambling up a gusty rock ridge.

A note on sizing: Since the Airshed Pro was originally designed for runners, the cut is quite slim. I size up to facilitate a wider range of motion.

I have been loving the Black Diamond Solution Hoody ($299) as a light fall belay jacket and even for cold rock climbing when I’m too chilly to shed my jacket. The Solution manages to be super lightweight, movement-friendly, and breathable, yet warm. This is largely because the specific PrimaLoft insulation used in this jacket is made with technology developed by NASA for astronauts. Here on earth, it also works great. It honestly does feel like being wrapped in warm air.

The pocket real estate is also generous, and it’s nice that the whole thing packs into the left hand pocket so you can easily stash it in your pack without a cam or crampon tearing into the insulation. I’m also looking forward to using this as a mid-layer below a shell for ice climbing this winter.

Unless you’ve been living under a rock (which many of our readers hopefully have), you’ve heard about the new film about the weed plane that crashed into a Yosemite lake in 1976. What followed was a tragicomic climber gold rush. Now, to mark the 50th anniversary of the incident, you can sport one of several new t-shirts or hats ($29.95-$44.95) memorializing this infamous moment in Yosemite climbing history. All proceeds support the Yosemite Climbing Association and the Yosemite Climbing Museum.

Have I used one? No. Do I desperately want one? Yes. As a 5’2” climber, I’m often just a few inches short of being able to make the clip from a good stance. I’ve heard wonderful things about the stiffy Kong Panic Draw ($54.95-$59.95) that lets you clip bolts otherwise out of reach. Maybe 2027 will be the year I finally gift myself one! If there’s a short or easily panicked sport climber in your life, I bet they would be stoked to get this as a present. Or you could make one for them DIY-style yourself.

The oversized, durable Metolius Climbers Tote ($64.95) has become my go-to gym bag. It easily fits my personal gear plus a 60m rope, or four sets of personal gear (shoes, harness, and chalkbag for me, my husband, and my two kids). Despite having stored weathered climbing shoes zippered inside for too long, this bag miraculously doesn’t stink yet either. It stows away nicely in gym cubbies (whereas my previous Cotopaxi tote would sort of spill out) and is easy to carry by hand or shoulder. This is a great mid-sized gift for the climber in your life, or you could fill it with other gear you’re gifting for a bigger impact.

Note on price: While this tote isn’t on sale at REI, co-op members get 20% off one full-price item through Nov. 30, so this is a good one to save on.

I receive a lot of climbing books and galleys to review and consider for coverage. Only some of them are so good that I can’t put them down. That’s how I felt about Erratica, a new collection of true stories from Moab-based, life-long climber Brian Laidlaw. Rare is the read that both keeps you attentive and entertained, yet also evokes deeper reflection. With Erratica, Brian manages to do just that. Pick up a copy ($20) at Moab’s Back of Beyond bookstore, or find one online anywhere books are sold.

Indian Actress and Director Nandita Das, Syrian Star Taim Hassan to Be Honored by Doha Film Festival

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Indian actress, director and social advocate Nandita Das and Syrian star Taim Hassan are set to be honored by the Doha Film Festival with Creative Excellence Awards during the event’s opening ceremony on Thursday.

Das has acted in more than 40 feature films, starting with Deepa Mehta’s “Fire,” which depicts a relationship between two women in an Indian household. She’s subsequently worked with top Indian directors such as Mrinal Sen, Shyam Benegal, Adoor Gopalakrishnan, Rituporno Ghosh and Mani Ratnam. Her directorial debut “Firaaq” — about the aftermath of the 2002 sectarian carnage in Gujarat, where 3,000 Muslims were killed — played at Toronto in 2008 and went on to travel widely and score many awards. She’s been a member of the main jury at Cannes Film Festival twice, in 2005 and 2013. Related Stories Gang Dong-won, Kim Go-eun to Star in Netflix’s 'The Twilight Between Us' From 'When Life Gives You Tangerines' Director, 'Queen of Tears' Writer 'Stranger Things' Prequel Spinoff 'Other Worlds' Ordered to Series at Netflix

Hassan made his breakthrough playing young ruler Fatimid Caliph Al-Adid in Syrian director Hatem Ali’s historical epics “Al-Zir Salem” (2000) and “Saladin” (2001). He has led all five seasons of hit Arabic TV show “Al Hayba,” about a family of arms smugglers working the border between Syria and Lebanon, a role that has made him a household name across the region and reached international audiences on Netflix. Hassan’s more recent work includes the show “Taj” (2024), in which he plays a boxer resisting the French occupation of Syria; hit political drama “Under the Seventh Ground” (2025); and thriller series “Mawlana” (2026).

“Cinema transcends borders, challenges perceptions and brings us closer to our shared humanity. Nandita Das and Taim Hassan represent the very best of this power,” said Doha Film Institute CEO Fatma Hassan Alremaihi, who also heads the festival. “Their remarkable artistry, courage of expression and unwavering commitment to authentic storytelling resonates far beyond geographical and cultural boundaries.”

As previously announced, Yemeni-Scottish filmmaker Sara Ishaq’s timely drama “The Station” — which looks at the impact of Yemen’s civil war on the country — is set to open the revamped event’s second edition, which will run Nov. 19-27 in the Qatari capital.

The Further Mis-Adventures Of Cliff Booth First Reactions Are Officially In

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth is taking on the dark side of Hollywood.

Quentin Tarantino's Oscar Award-winning movie has finally got a sequel with David Fincher taking over as director. The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth, starring Brad Pitt, has finally reached some screens with critics weighing in on whether the sequel is a worthy follow-up to Once Upon a Time in...Hollywood. First reactions for the gritty dark comedy have officially arrived ahead of its limited theatrical release.

Critic Zach Pope shared his initial thoughts after watching The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth for the first time and stated that the movie "is a PURE cinematic trifecta of Tarantino’s signature chaos, Fincher’s meticulous attention to detail, and Brad Pitt’s effortlessly charismatic Cliff Booth." Pope continued and shared that:

"All wrapped into a stylish love letter to 1970s Hollywood cinema that reminds us sometimes all you need is a laid back, wildly entertaining misadventure with a character you just LOVE spending time with. That just might turn into insanity.

It’s cool, chaotic, charming, and never tries to be more than it needs to be. If you loved Once Upon a Time in Hollywood, this one’s absolutely for you. Just as it was for me!

But Elizabeth Debicki STEALS the entire movie. What a performance."

Host Clayton Davis posted his early reaction to Cliff Booth on X and praised the likes of Pitt for his "best performance since Once Upon a Time in...Hollywood." Davis continued and said, "Elizabeth Debicki brings an intoxicating sensuality, and Scott Caan and Peter Weller are such a treat that they could have had triple the screen time. The cinematography is gorgeous. If the 2019 film was a love letter to L.A., this one is a full-on sonnet. It’s also a nice reminder that not every director needs to write their own films, and not every writer needs to direct them. Fincher and Tarantino haven’t been this much fun in years."

Journalist Drew Taylor wants everyone to go watch The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth now as he shares, "Absolutely loved #CliffBooth — Tarantino and Fincher are like peanut butter and chocolate (the peanut butter is profane and the chocolate dipped in blood). Stop watching anything, go see it on the biggest screen around, have the time of your life. An absolute triumph. The best."

Film and TV critic Tessa Smith calls Once Upon a Time in...Hollywood's sequel "a violent, bloody, absolute blast," and continues by adding, "A love letter to Hollywood and the entertainment industry, it was so fun seeing so many popular LA locations. Brad Pitt, as expected, is phenomenal! Definitely see it in @IMAX!!!"

The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth is clearly making a good impression, as Collider's Steven Weintraub gave a similarly positive reaction to the upcoming release by stating, "Loved The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth. Huge surprise that QT + Fincher+ Brad Pitt would make something awesome. I love a good hangout movie and this delivered everything I wanted and more. Absolutely see this in a movie theater! So many things I want to talk about but don't want to spoil anything."

Senior reporter Germain Lussier shared that, "THE FURTHER MIS-ADVENTURES OF CLIFF BOOTH is my favorite movie of the year and it’s not even close. Hilarious, exciting, romantic, you’ll never guess what happens next and probably not believe it then anyway. Couldn’t love it more if I tried."

Actor and critic Josh Blumenkranz also saw the Tarantino and Fincher sequel before its release and stated, "THE FURTHER MISADVENTURES OF CLIFF BOOTH a hilarious, violent, and cool hangout movie. Brad Pitt is charming as ever as Cliff Booth. Welcome additions Elizabeth Debicki and Scott Caan also deliver. Not as good as Once but still a fun time. Love letter to Los Angeles! #cliffbooth"

Rupert Grint Jokes About Reuniting With Daniel Radcliffe and Emma Watson: “I’ll Make Some Calls”

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

In Ebenezer, director Ti West’s take on Charles Dickens’ Christmastime classic, Harry Potter alum Rupert Grint and Emmy Award-winning Severence star Tramell Tillman are stepping into the shoes of two well-loved literary characters, Bob Cratchit and the Ghost of Christmas Present, respectively. They join a stacked ensemble with Paramount Pictures, alongside Johnny Depp in the title role, bringing a new spin on the timeless tale.

In Ebenezer, we revisit one of the most well-known ghost stories of all time, A Christmas Carol, in a thrilling new on-screen adaptation that stars Ian McKellen as Jacob Marley, Sam Claflin as Fred, Daisy Ridley as Clara, Andrea Riseborough as the Ghost of Christmas Past, and Robert Strange as the Ghost of Christmas Future, as well as Arthur Conti as a young Ebenezer Scrooge, and Ellie Bamber as Belle.

While in London, Collider’s Steve Weintraub had the opportunity to chat with Grint and Tillman about their time on set sharing scenes with the “unpredictable” Depp, and what they learned working opposite the Oscar nominee. Plus, Grint comments on reprising his role as Ron Weasley in the Broadway production of Harry Potter and the Cursed Child, and the pair play a game of “Save or Kill,” choosing between some of pop culture’s most legendary movies, television, and creators.

COLLIDER: I just found out that you're going to go over to Broadway and play Ron Weasley again in Harry Potter. What made you say yes to that? Was it something you were thinking about?

RUPERT GRINT: Yeah. It's funny. It's interesting, going back. I spent so much time after finishing Potter kind of moving away from it and doing other things, which I've really enjoyed doing, but I think now it's a very different role in a very different context. And weirdly, it's kind of lined up that I'm the exact age of the character in that story, so it's just felt like a good time.

And I love New York and being on stage. It's been a while since I've done that, and it's a very important thing to me. It was my childhood, and I have a very strong connection to that character and that world. I think I'm not going to get many other opportunities to play him again. It probably is my last, and I think it'll be fun.

GRINT: [Laughs] Well, yeah, it's very nostalgic. Even for me, it is. It's fun to go back and get back into that world. It's been a while.

When you agreed to do it, did you think about picking up the phone and calling Daniel [Radcliffe] and Emma [Watson], and being like, “Hey, you wanna do a few weeks on Broadway and make people go crazy?”

GRINT: It would be fun. I don't know. I mean, it's obviously different for everyone. It was an intense time. When we finished, I really enjoyed stepping away and not really even thinking about it, because it was such an intense… I mean, it was enjoyable, and they looked after us, but it was a big part of us. So, yeah, maybe. I don't know. It'd be fun.

Seeing all of you guys on stage together, people would lose their minds, including myself.

GRINT: I'll make it happen. I'll make some calls. [Laughs]

I do have an individual for you. I am a huge fan of Severance, as you know. Have you wrapped filming on Season 3?

I got it. I'll leave it there. Are you excited by the scripts?

A new post from creator Dan Erickson brings even more hope about the turnaround between seasons.

The other thing I’m excited to do with you guys today is I'm bringing back a game I haven't played in 10 years. It's called Save or Kill, and basically one of these things can be saved, one is erased from existence, and you have to pick. They're all kind of brutal choices because they're equal. We're going to start with Pretty Woman or Dirty Dancing.

GRINT: Well, I'm going to pick Pretty Woman.

TILLMAN: [Sings] “I’ve had the time of my life…” I mean, you can’t…

GRINT: Yeah, you’d lose that dance. The lift.

TILLMAN: That dance moment. The lift, which lives on at weddings everywhere. But, I mean, Pretty Woman is…

All the things I'm going to give you are on that level.

TILLMAN: Okay, I’m going to save Dirty Dancing if you’re going to save Pretty Woman.

GRINT: Oh, interesting. It’s close. I’ll save Pretty Woman.

TILLMAN: Okay, I've never seen Goodfellas. I have to be honest.

That's one of my all-time favorite films. It’s phenomenal.

I think you're going to be saving Pulp Fiction then.

TILLMAN: Okay, yeah. I’ll be saving Pulp Fiction.

TILLMAN: It's only because I haven't seen the film.

GRINT: I think that's tricky because I do love the clown line. But yeah, I'm saving Pulp, as well.

TILLMAN: Hitchcock. I’ve got to save Hitchcock. I grew up watching Hitchcock.

GRINT: I don't know if I'm swayed by your shirt.

This one's a little tough. Quentin Tarantino or David Fincher?

GRINT: That's tricky. I do like Tarantino. He's given me a lot of joy.

TILLMAN: He’s always been good. I'm leaning towards Tarantino, but I love David Fincher.

There's no right or wrong. Steven Spielberg or Christopher Nolan.

GRINT: I haven’t watched a lot of Seinfeld. I've seen clips, and I like what I've seen.

GRINT: I like his stand-up, but, yeah, Friends is very nostalgic for me.

TILLMAN: Seinfeld is more nostalgic for me, so I would save Seinfeld.

TILLMAN: Save Indiana Jones for me. Again, nostalgic.

GRINT: I would say the same, actually. Though, the first Star Wars is pretty… Yeah, I'll agree.

GRINT: I’m ashamed to say this — I've never seen Breaking Bad.

Oh, sir, you need to make some time in your schedule before you start doing Harry Potter.

TILLMAN: You would dig it. I think you would dig into that.

GRINT: Oh, that was good, as well. But I do want to watch Breaking Bad. I'll save it because I want to see it.

TILLMAN: Alright, okay. Yes. Sopranos. I'll say The Sopranos.

GRINT: Yeah, that's quite easy for me, actually.

TILLMAN: I love the Stones. Okay, Stones! I gotta go Stones.

This is an easy one. Queen or Led Zeppelin?

I was being sarcastic, by the way. They're both incredible.

I'll do one more. Michael Jackson or Prince.

TILLMAN: [Laughs] Oh, goddogit. Michael. I’m saving Michael.

TILLMAN: I'm sorry, Prince. I’m saving Michael!

Jumping into the film. I'm a huge fan of Johnny. I've watched him work on set, and I've noticed he likes to do different things on different takes. Can you talk about what surprised you about working with him and what you took away from the experience? Because he's been doing this for a while, and he's just a very, very talented individual in front of the camera.

GRINT: He was amazing to watch. Just watching him transform, he is one of those actors that completely disappears into a character. And so much mischievous work, as well. He's always trying to find a gag in every kind of moment and some levity, which was really fun. And then you never really know what he's going to do. He’s quite an unpredictable person to be on set with, which I really enjoyed.

TILLMAN: I would have to say, with all of that, his generosity. There are actors that I've worked with that are so talented, but everything is for them. He's very generous when it comes to the craft and the art, and so you don't feel as though this is just his show. You feel like you're a part of it. He’s including you in the moment. And the gags that he may play never supersede or take away from your work, as well. It's a part of the story.

I could never do what you guys do. I'm just very impressed by actors, especially on your level, and I'm very curious, do you have a pre-scene ritual, something that you do to get you ready in the mental headspace before the camera starts going?

TILLMAN: Well, I have a playlist for my characters that I've played, so I tend to jam out. That helps me get into the mood of the shot. But I tend to, in order to get out of my head, I just check in with people, like say hi to the crew and ask them how their day was, because I can get very locked in. But just to kind of touch grass a little bit and remember that there are other people around, that helps me stay present.

GRINT: That’s important, I think. But also, in something like this, a period piece where it's so atmospheric, the sets and the costumes, you're wearing so many layers, and the shoes, you kind of really do get lost in it, and I think that’s really helpful to feel like you're part of that time. I find that useful.

‘Miss Scarlet’ Replacement Officially Gets First Look

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

For six years, Miss Scarlet has quietly proven to be one of the best period dramas on television. The story of Victorian London’s first female private detective has been packed with intrigue, as Kate Phillips' Eliza Scarlet, Tom Durant-Pritchard's Inspector Alexander Blake, and the rest of the cast treated audiences to unmissable, winding historical crime drama. The most recent sixth season drew another batch of positive reviews, including an 8/10 rating from Collider's Jen Vestuto, who praised Season 6 for "delivering some of its sharpest writing yet."

Alas, all good things must come to an end. The conclusion of Season 6 was followed by the news that an upcoming seventh installment would be the show's last. "It’s been a privilege to work on a show crafted with so much love and dedication and as we prepare to say goodbye, I’m so proud of what we’ve achieved and the memories we’ve made," said star Phillips in a statement, as the reality of just one more season of this beloved show kicks in for fans.

But fear not, those afraid of a life without Miss Scarlet, as creators Rachael New and Ben Edwards have today announced a brand-new project to fill the gap. Titled Fleet Street, this new period crime drama will air on PBS MASTERPIECE and is a co-production by Mikey Productions Limited and MASTERPIECE. Executive Producers are New, Edwards, Patricia Lenahan, and Patrick Irwin for Mikey Productions Limited and Susanne Simpson for MASTERPIECE. A synopsis for the series reads:

"Fleet Street begins in 1901. Queen Victoria is dead, and all the newspapers are racing to capture a nation at the end of an era. The death of a florist with ties to the Royal Household gives Kitty and Samuel their first lead. A missing son, an anonymous message, and a violent death draw them into an investigation reaching far beyond the newsroom. With the Queen’s funeral approaching, powerful people have good reason to keep the truth out of print. The story could make Kitty’s career and restore Samuel’s—or put them both in danger."

Four criminal masterminds. One of them was always going to be yours. Here is who comes for you, why they picked you out of everyone available, and what they want from the encounter.

Your nemesis does not want to beat you. He wants to know you completely, and he is patient enough to wait for it. The frightening part is how good the conversation is.

Your nemesis is not after money, territory or revenge. He is after a demonstration, and you are the person he has chosen to prove his point on.

Your nemesis respects you, which sounds flattering until you work out what it means. He chose you because nobody else was worth his time.

Your nemesis has no personal interest in you whatsoever, and that is precisely what makes him unsurvivable. You are not a rival. You are a stop on the way.

Described as "cinematic, romantic and alive with the energy of London’s high-stakes world of journalism at the dawn of a new century," Fleet Street is set to begin filming soon in Belgrade, Serbia, with six episodes greenlit. Starring in the series are Silvia Presente, known for portraying Jane Eleanora Pocock in Outlander, and Guy Remmers, who starred in Apple TV's The Buccaneers.

Presente's Kitty Parker is described as "a young woman from a wealthy family who travels to London from her countryside home determined to become a journalist." She will star opposite Remmers, who will play Samuel Wallace, "a journalist at The Morning Herald. Once one of Fleet Street’s brightest reporters, he is now struggling professionally and haunted by the tragic consequences of a past story."

For more news, make sure to stay tuned to Collider.

Guy Ritchie & Jason Statham's Best Movie Together Is Officially Streaming For Free

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Guy Ritchie is a beloved director with an expansive filmography. He's arguably best known for his crime stories and stylish approach to storytelling. Those crime tales feature larger-than-life characters, with slick writing that's often endlessly quotable.

Lock, Stock & Two Smoking Barrels is a great example of the formula, and feels like the blueprint for several other Guy Ritchie projects. The movie is about four friends who lose a rigged poker game. They end up owing a massive gambling debt to Hatchet Harry (P.H. Moriarty), with just a week to pay it back.

While the movie does focus on how the friends intend to pay off their debt, Ritchie takes the time to flesh out so many characters. Rory Breaker (Vas Blackwood), for example, plays more of a supporting role, but is also responsible for one of the funniest speeches in the entire movie.

Lock, Stock & Two Smoking Barrels is also a remarkable project for Jason Statham, as it was his feature film debut. Since then, Statham has appeared in numerous other Guy Ritchie movies, along with dozens of other projects with other directors. He's widely regarded as a modern action star, and Snatch, arguably his best movie directed by Ritchie, is now free to stream on Pluto TV.

Jason Statham played Bacon in Lock, Stock & Two Smoking Barrels. He is a main character and one of the four friends who lost in Hatchet Harry's poker game. Statham also stars in Ritchie's 2000 crime caper, Snatch. He plays Turkish, an illegal boxing promoter working alongside his close friend, Tommy (Stephen Graham).

The movie has the same storytelling style as Lock, Stock & Two Smoking Barrels, with several tales intertwining into something far grander. Turkish and Tommy run into trouble when their prizefighter is injured. Unfortunately for the duo, they had a fight arranged with another promoter and feared crime boss, Brick Top (Alan Ford). He's furious the fight can no longer take place, and fully capitalizes on the situation.

Turkish and Tommy are at the center of the story, but they are just one part of what makes Snatch a classic. Vinnie Jones was also in Lock, Stock & Two Smoking Barrels, and returns as Bullet Tooth Tony. His story is only loosely connected to the illegal boxing plot, but he remains an unforgettable character, responsible for some ridiculously over-the-top speeches.

Lock, Stock & Two Smoking Barrels is an adored crime caper. It has such a well-crafted story that even non-crime fans will find something to enjoy, but Snatch is arguably even better. The smaller stories in Snatch fit together masterfully, and while Turkish and Tommy are the main characters, they're only part of what makes the movie so charming.

Another major element of Snatch is a diamond heist that occurs at the start of the movie. The diamond ends up in London, but its arrival also draws the attention of mobsters like Boris the Blade (Rade Sherbedgia). The dialogue throughout the entire movie is arguably wittier and funnier than its predecessor, and no character in the extensive cast feels underutilized.

There are plenty of serious moments, but some of the sharp-tongued exchanges are exceptional. Brick Top, in particular, is a treasure trove of quotes, and some of his speeches are arguably among the best quotes in Guy Ritchie's entire filmography.

Lock, Stock, and Two Smoking Barrels will always deserve a special mention for its originality. Snatch feels like it builds on the proven formula. The story is more complex, yet it's not convoluted. Many of the characters are wonderfully grandiose, and somehow still convincing. The soundtrack will always be a personal preference, but several music choices really are perfect in Snatch. This is particularly true for the climactic boxing match, as "Fin' in the Bushes" by Oasis leads us nicely to one of the greatest characters in the entire movie.

Without delving too deep into spoilers, Brad Pitt plays Mickey, a wild traveler in Snatch. Turkish sends Tommy to meet Mickey and complete a transaction for a new caravan. The deal goes south, but the duo discovers that Mickey is actually a bare-knuckle boxing champion. They want him to fight for them, but he's unpredictable and will only play along for a price.

Pitt's Mickey is unforgettable in Snatch. He certainly looks tough with his shirt off, but he steals every scene he's in with some of the funniest dialogue in the entire movie. His accent is over the top, and he's purposefully difficult to understand.

Best of all, Pitt seems to be enjoying playing the character. His mannerisms are believable for such an over-the-top character, and he's so likable because audiences never know what he'll do next. Mickey also becomes embroiled in Turkish and Tommy's situation with Brick Top, and his chemistry with the rest of the cast is exceptional. He's arguably the biggest star in the movie, with an incredible screen presence, and a perfect actor for the role.

Since Lock, Stock & Two Smoking Barrels, Jason Statham has become a staple of sorts in Guy Ritchie movies. Statham starred in 2005's Revolver after Snatch. He also starred in Wrath of Man in 2021, a notably more serious, gritty revenge movie, wherein Statham plays H, a mysterious man of few words who starts working for a security truck company. It's a high-risk job, but H proves deadly efficient. He also has a unique motivation for joining that we learn about as the story unfolds.

Most recently, Statham teamed up with Ritchie for 2023's Operation Fortune: Ruse de Guerre. The movie is a spy thriller, starring Statham as Orson, an elite agent. The story is notably less complex than Snatch, for example, but still boasts a brilliant cast, including Josh Hartnett and Aubrey Plaza. Hugh Grant is also unforgettable as Greg, a powerful arms dealer.

Audiences who enjoy Ritchie and Statham's collaborations will have to wait until January 2028 for their next fix. Details on their upcoming movie are scarce, but there are already some details about Viva La Madness to get excited about. Statham is reportedly the lead, and Vinnie Jones is also part of the cast alongside Camila Mendes and Jason Isaacs.

Interestingly, Viva La Madness is a crime novel written by J.J. Connolly. That novel is also a sequel to Layer Cake, which had a movie adaptation in 2004, starring Daniel Craig. Both novels are about a London criminal called X, though it's unclear whether the upcoming Viva La Madness movie will be connected to Layer Cake. Regardless, the plot and setting alone should be enough to get fans of Snatch and Lock, Stock & Two Smoking Barrels excited, especially given Jason Stathan and Guy Ritchie's involvement.

Robert Pattinson‘s casting as James Bond has received a nod of polite acknowledgement from the British monarchy.

Roughly 90 minutes after the news broke that Pattinson was to lead Amazon MGM Studios reboot of the hit franchise, the royals joined hundreds of thousands of commentators on social media — but did so in a manner that really only they could.

“Welcome to His Majesty’s Secret Service, Mr Bond,” they posted on the royal family‘s official X account, with a link to the announcement. Related Stories 'Blue Eye Samurai' Sets Season 2 Release Date at Netflix Alex de la Iglesia on His Upcoming Chilean Shoot ‘The Misunderstanding’: ‘Reality Has Become My Parallel World’

As it happens, Pattinson will be the very first Bond to work for His Majesty’s Secret Service rather than Her Majesty’s Secret Service. Queen Elizabeth II was monarch across the entire slate of 25 titles until her passing in 2022, a year after the release of “No Time to Die,” the last 007 movie and Daniel Craig’s final stint as the super spy.

Welcome to His Majesty's Secret Service, Mr Bond. https://t.co/inKwKqHEDd

Pattinson’s casting brought to a close years of speculation over who incoming Bond director Denis Villenueve would hand the famed Walther PKK, speculation that turned into a frenzy over the last month as the names were whittled down. Jacob Elordi was initially a favorite for the role in the earliest days, but reports later surfaced that the “Euphoria” star didn’t even screen test for the part. Jack Lowden then entered the fray, with his “Slow Horses” co-star Gary Oldman sparking headlines when he claimed Bond was already picked and cheekily added that his “fingers are crossed” for Lowden. Callum Turner spent a lengthy period as a candidate on many lips and was reportedly down to the final stages. But then the tables were turned at the 11th hour when it emerged broke that both Pattinson and Nicholas Hoult had auditioned.

“James Bond is one of the most legendary characters in cinema, and to be entrusted with 007 is the honor of a lifetime,” Pattinson said in a statement. “I’m deeply grateful to Denis, Amy, David, Tanya and everyone at Amazon MGM Studios for their belief in me. To say this role is a dream come true is an understatement. I couldn’t be more excited about this partnership and the work ahead with this fantastic team.”

52 Years Later, One Of Sci-Fi's Most Legendary Space Operas Finally Gets Its Streaming Finale

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

One of pop culture's longest-running space operas celebrates the end of an era. The space opera genre is most famously represented by Star Wars, the movie-turned-franchise that overhauled sci-fi cinema with its revolutionary use of VFX and SFX before creating a massive multimedia empire that only continues to grow half a century later. Dune became a phenomenon of its own earlier than Star Wars, though it only met its full potential thirty-seven years after its first movie adaptation and fifty-six years after the first novel's publication. On television, Star Trek pioneered optimistic cosmic exploration and Battlestar Galactica brought survival-driven drama to extraterrestrial fleet warfare.

Tucked beneath those household names, dozens of underrated space operas still await the mainstream recognition they deserve. The Jim Henson Company's Farscape is a unique title in the genre due to its high-quality puppetry, and Babylon 5 popularized serialized five-year television storytelling long before the streaming era. In animation, Titan A.E., Crest of the Stars, and Outlaw Star delivered heartfelt starfaring adventures that often get overshadowed by bigger franchises. There are also countless literary gems like Vernor Vinge’s A Fire Upon the Deep, Lois McMaster Bujold’s Vorkosigan Saga, and Arkady Martine’s A Memory Called Empire.

Right alongside Star Wars and Star Trek, a fan-favorite space opera reaches a new milestone half a century after its first installment.

Fifty-two years after the original 1974 series, one of anime's most influential space operas has delivered its complete streaming finale with all twenty-six episodes of Star Blazers: Space Battleship Yamato 3199. The series' final chapter weaves together the 1980 theatrical feature Be Forever Yamato and the third classic television season, Space Battleship Yamato III, into a cohesive new timeline. Set in the year 2207, Star Blazers: Space Battleship Yamato 3199 follows Susumu Kodai and the scattered veterans of the Yamato as they reunite in secret after a colossal structure called the Grand Reverse breaches Earth's defenses and allows the enigmatic Dezarium army to occupy the capital.

Star Blazers: Space Battleship Yamato 3199 presents a striking blend of classic mechanical designs and mature military drama that respects decades of canon and keeps veteran fans guessing at every turn, plus sweeping orchestral music that makes the space opera feel even more majestic. The seventh and final chapter adds episodes 23 through 26 to the catalog after its October 2026 theatrical debut in Japan. With the entire twenty-six-episode arc all available at once, newcomers and longtime admirers alike can experience the complete saga without interruption after more than fourteen years of anticipation.

Star Blazers: Space Battleship Yamato 3199 brings classic space opera back to its roots with every classic space opera trope that originally inspired modern sci-fi icons like Star Wars and Battlestar Galactica. Throughout its seven chapters, 3199 delivers daring espionage behind enemy lines and thundering fleet battles led by captain Susumu Kodai, tactical ace Yuki Mori, veteran tactician Shiro Sanada, and the rapidly growing Sasha alongside the menacing Dezarium commander Alphon. Standout installments like the tense opening invasion in Chapter 1, the underground resistance intrigue of Chapter 3, and the breathtaking battles in Chapters 6 and 7 showcase breathtaking choreography and Akira Miyagawa's lush orchestral score.

If you love Star Trek, Captain Kirk’s ongoing series needs to be on your radar, especially if you’re a lore fanatic.

Now that all twenty-six episodes of 3199 have wrapped up the series, the future of the Space Battleship Yamato franchise looks remarkably bright as it enters its second half-century. Since launching the modern remake continuity with Yamato 2199 in 2012, studios like Xebec, Satelight, and Studio Mother have reimagined the classic 1970s and early-1980s saga to immense critical and commercial success. Given the warm reception to 3199 and chief director Harutoshi Fukui's clear passion for the material, a new installment could present a modern reimagining of the 1983 theatrical epic Final Yamato and its legendary Planet Aquarius crisis alongside the upcoming movie Cosmoship Yamato √2199.

Scott has a few exciting projects on the horizon, though, not least of which being his new Treasure Island reboot with Hugh Jackman. Last year, Scott took his producing talents to Apple TV to work on the electric psychological thriller, Echo Valley, which stars Sydney Sweeney opposite Julianne Moore. It joins the ranks of other Apple TV smash hits like The Gorge and Greyhound that have managed to hit this colossal milestone this year.

Four criminal masterminds. One of them was always going to be yours. Here is who comes for you, why they picked you out of everyone available, and what they want from the encounter.

Your nemesis does not want to beat you. He wants to know you completely, and he is patient enough to wait for it. The frightening part is how good the conversation is.

Your nemesis is not after money, territory or revenge. He is after a demonstration, and you are the person he has chosen to prove his point on.

Your nemesis respects you, which sounds flattering until you work out what it means. He chose you because nobody else was worth his time.

Your nemesis has no personal interest in you whatsoever, and that is precisely what makes him unsurvivable. You are not a rival. You are a stop on the way.

“In the edge-of-your-seat thriller Echo Valley, Kate (Julianne Moore) is a mother struggling to make peace with her troubled daughter Claire (Sydney Sweeney), a situation that becomes even more perilous when Claire shows up on Kate's doorstep, hysterical and covered in someone else's blood. As Kate pieces together the shocking truth of what happened, she learns just how far a mother will go to try to save her child in this gripping tale of love, sacrifice and survival from BAFTA-winning director Michael Pearce and Emmy-nominated writer Brad Ingelsby.”

‘The Odyssey’ Cinematographer Hoyte Van Hoytema Breaks Down Filming Climactic Thunderstorm Scene in Imax

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Christopher Nolan shot “The Odyssey” to be experienced on the biggest screen possible so audiences could feel the full impact and be fully immersed in that experience — so everything was shot practically.

One of the most challenging sequences to pull off was the film’s final storm scene as Matt Damon’s Odysseus faces the wrath of Poseidon and the full force of Zeus. It was to be climactic and intense, and Odysseus loses all his men as a result of the final insult to the gods. Related Stories 'The Body' Creator on the Dance Team's Sexuality, What Happened to Grace and Whether God Is Real Keira Knightley and 'Black Doves' Creator Joe Barton on Why Helen Should Have Been the One to Kill [SPOILER], That Oner and Hopes for Season 3

“We had been talking about going out there to sea, sit on real boats and shoot on Imax,” cinematographer Hoyte Van Hoytema explains.

A large amount of the film was shot on the open ocean, where Nolan, his cast and crew spent four months capturing how hard the journey would have been as they endured crashing waves. And while the climactic moment ended up being shot in Los Angeles, Nolan didn’t spare any expense in pulling off the sequence, relying instead on old-fashioned filmmaking.Speaking with Variety for Inside the Frame, Van Hoytema explains the scene provided a challenge: “How to replicate something and make it feel visceral and real? How can we do that and avoid a huge amount of CG?”Two decommissioned jet engines were used as wind machines. Van Hoytema’s chief lighting technician and gaffer Adam Chambers built light boxes that would emulate thunder and lightning. The boat was then put on a gimbal, all while being shot on an Imax camera. “It was like shooting a real storm, just under safety supervision,” Van Hoytema says.He adds, “We did a lot of different setups in that whole scene. It is designed to feel extremely chaotic; it’s designed to sort of not understand really what’s going on.”The lighting design was something he also had to think about. “It was interesting because I was thinking, if you’re out at sea at night 2,000 years ago, without electricity and you end up in a thunderstorm, what would that feel like? I was thinking, if you can see everything around you, that’s going to look fake.”

A CGI storm didn’t feel right because the entire crew had been meticulous about keeping everything as grounded and as experiential as possible. Van Hoytema says the team built overhead lighting rigs that were approximately 60×30 feet, hanging on cranes above the ship. “It meant that you would see flashes that progressively move from behind them to the front of them, as it opens up the sky. “What you see is short bursts and there’s just darkness or a little bit of a glow. It’s like trying to see this relentless endless sea, this weather, this saltwater and they’re just so close to death. It’s extremely claustrophobic at the same time.”

When it came to framing Odysseus, Van Hoytema was wired in to keep him in place on the gimbal. His Imax camera on his shoulder was also wired in.

“I was not trying to be in any way precious about framing whatsoever. I was just trying through with a jet engine blowing, gallons of water in my face. I was literally just trying to identify if it was even Matt Damon in the shot. It was extremely visceral, and it’s almost hard to grasp. It was not a situation in which you could just start a take or not. Every take was an event.”

Van Hoytema says the camera starts to roll and the jet engines start. “They rise, and at some point, they sound like two fighter jets are next to your ear. And it gets really wild because they start moving the gimbal as well, and you’re holding on for your life. The camera gets turned; it’s the original Imax camera, not the blimp and the Imax camera is very loud. However, in that situation, you don’t even know if it’s turned on. You do a take, and you have a crazy powerful adrenaline rush.”

In contrast, Van Hoytema recalled a moment when the crew was out to sea, in open waters and a pod of bottlenose dolphins appeared. It was completely unplanned.

Van Hoytema recalls, “It’s interesting because it was also pretty violent that day. It was very harsh weather and I remember a lot of people on the crew got seasick. As we started filming, dolphins started to jump out of the water. What is interesting is that it symbolizes that no matter what the weather does, we take the weather as it comes and accept it as the reality you work in that day.”

James Bond Fans React To Robert Pattinson's 007 Casting

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Robert Pattinson has spent his career ping-ponging between major franchise fare ("Harry Potter," "Twilight," "The Batman") and weird arthouse fare ("The Lighthouse," "Good Time," "Cosmopolis"). Along the way, he's garnered a pretty broad fanbase, so there are plenty of people celebrating the news that Pattinson will be taking over the role of James Bond.

There were several other finalists to play the next Bond, including Josh O'Connor, Jack Lowden, and Nicholas Hoult, but Pattinson was the clear frontrunner even before he was announced. Early reactions to the news have noted the somewhat absurd flex of landing the role of James Bond just a few years after landing the role of Batman. "This is the greed they talk about in the Bible," wrote one user on Reddit.

Casting such a well-known actor in the role of Bond bucks the trend of 007 casting, with previous revamps traditionally favoring actors who weren't already firmly established. Many people, while not necessarily disliking the choice of Pattinson for the role, have pointed out that Amazon isn't exactly taking a risk. Other Reddit users describe Pattinson as "a very conservative choice" and "very safe casting," with one wryly commenting, "This isn't beating the allegations of 'every movie has the same five actors in it.'" So, while casting a lesser-known actor would surely have attracted some controversy in its own right, some Bond fans feel that the safety of this choice is "disappointing."

And, of course, there are Batman fans who fear that this fresh commitment spells the end of Pattinson's tenure as the Dark Knight. Between production delays on "The Batman II," a recent power shake-up at DC Studios, and now this 007 casting news, the general assumption is that Pattinson's Batman will be hanging up his cape after two movies.

One of the upsides of having such an eccentric career and personality is that Robert Pattinson has generated a great many memes over the years — and now they're being put to good use. One Tumblr user adapted a famous "Twilight" scene to have Pattinson's Edward Cullen saying, "This is the skin of a [license to kill]er," Bella." Others, meanwhile, have joked that we "finally have a Gen Z James Bond," in reference to a Vanity Fair interview wherein Pattinson doggedly insists that he is Gen Z and that the year he was born "doesn't matter."

One meme that became popular even before Pattinson was officially cast imagines the reactions of the other hopefuls auditioning for the role when they see the unstoppable Pattinson walk in after them.

every other actor when robert pattinson shows up to audition pic.twitter.com/40fHsnS2C4

— ivy (@ohhhhhherewegoo) November 16, 2026

Fans are particularly amused by the fact that Pattinson showed up "late" to the audition process (which has been going on for years), sandwiching it in between his commitments to "Dune: Part Three" and "The Batman Part II," and still landed the role. But that kind of effortless swagger only makes him more perfect for the role of James Bond.

Vikings won't be leaving Minnesota for altitude acclimation

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

With quarterback Kirk Cousins back and the Minnesota defense playing at a championship level, the sky is the limit for the Vikings.

On Sunday, the extent to which they’ll be literally in the sky could limit the ability to get an eighth win.

The Week 11 prime-time showdown between the 7-2 Vikings and 8-1 49ers will happen in Mexico City, where the altitude is 7,280 feet above sea level. The Vikings won’t be relocating this week to acclimate to the thinner air.

The 49ers, in contrast, have gone from Dallas to Colorado, where they’ll train in the far lower distance of 5,280 feet above sea level.

The Vikings will have a plan to get their players ready to perform on an elevated field. Details will emerge this week.

Whatever the plan is, it won’t entail the same kind of direct acclimation in which the 49ers are engaging.

It sets up another apples-to-apples comparison regarding the plans for international games. In Week 1, the 49ers went to Australia early. The Rams went late. And the 49ers dominated L.A., winning 27-7.

This time around, the stakes are extremely high. The winner takes over the de facto driver’s seat for the top seed in the NFC.

A loss means more to the Vikings than the 49ers, as it relates to their respective divisions. San Francisco has a solid cushion over the Seahawks and Rams in the NFC West. The Vikings are caught in a three-way tie for first place in the NFC North.

EXCLUSIVE: Over the summer, Universal unleased one menacing mommy upon theaters — and now, Lionsgate is preparing to unleash another. Dave Franco and Willa Fitzgerald have signed on to star in Mommy’s Home, the studio’s new psychological horror film to be directed by David F. Sandberg.

Details as to the duo’s roles are being kept under wraps. Sandberg, the director of Annabelle: Creation and Lights Out, helms the story of a young dad whose family life becomes terrifyingly unhinged when his cryogenically preserved mom is unfrozen and sent to live with him and his family. Related Stories Casting Aaron Pierre And 'Sisu' Director Jalmari Helander Team on Action Thriller 'Fairytale In New York' From Netflix And Lionsgate Acquisitions Lionsgate Acquires Alma Har'el's 'Wet' Starring Connor Storrie, Rebecca Ferguson, Javier Bardem, Odessa A'zion, Rosalía & Lukas Haas

Franco and Fitzgerald reteam after co-starring in Paramount’s 2025 Colleen Hoover adaptation Regretting You. Watch on Deadline

The script for Mommy’s Home is by James Morosini, who was attached to direct when we announced the project back in 2024. Producers include Craig Flores, Dawn Olmstead and Anonymous Content, along with Sandberg and Lotta Losten via Mångata. Nathan Kahane and Morosini will exec produce, as will Franco, joined by David Levine and Garrett Kemble on behalf of Anonymous Content.

Scott O’Brien and Maria Ascanio are overseeing the project for Lionsgate. Dan Freedman negotiated the deal on behalf of the studio, which launched international sales out of this year’s AFM.

Lionsgate Motion Picture Group President Erin Westerman told us, “Every once in a while, a script comes along that is so wildly original and impossible to forget that you just have to make it. Mommy’s Home is one of those movies. David F. Sandberg is an exceptional horror filmmaker, and we can’t imagine anyone better suited to bring this particular brand of madness to life.”

Westerman added, “Dave Franco is one of those rare actors who is endlessly watchable and impossible not to root for, even when the world around him is going completely off the rails. He’s been part of the Lionsgate family through Now You See Me, and we couldn’t be happier to be working with him again. And Willa Fitzgerald is going to be a revelation in this film. This is a project that everyone involved is making out of sheer passion, and we can’t wait for audiences to experience just how far it goes.”

Coming off the hard-R Sundance comedy Idiots and DreamWorks’ Forgotten Island, Franco will soon be seen reprising as himself in Season 2 of The Studio. This follows his Emmy nomination for a scene-stealing turn as himself in the CinemaCon-set Season 1 finale. He is repped by WME, Anonymous Content and Toczek Suddleson.

Breaking out with her turn in JT Mollner’s lauded psychological thriller Strange Darling, Fitzgerald has also been seen starring in Season 1 of the hit Amazon series Reacher, as well as Kathryn Bigelow’s Netflix film A House of Dynamite and Regretting You. Upcoming, she’ll be seen in the espionage thriller Billion Dollar Spy, as well as the Sony horror film Skeletons from Strange Darling‘s Mollner and producer J.J. Abrams. She is repped by CAA, Untitled and Jackoway Austen Tyerman.
