Dystopian Thriller ‘The Flaw’ Is a Cinematic Puzzle About Totalitarianism: “Each One of Us Is a Regime”

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

David Dawson is an interrogator of The Regime, and Arthur McBain is a suspect in a cat-and-mouse game in Theophilos Papastylianos' Tallinn-premiering debut feature, based on a bestselling novel.

In a dark, dystopian future, the Regime decides who belongs and who doesn’t. And the Regime puts the cold and calculating Interrogator on a man who becomes the Suspect to prove his guilt. He puts a ruthless plan of psychological manipulation into action. But could there be a shortcoming in the master plan?

The dystopian thriller The Flaw, a Greece–Latvia co-production, is based on Antonis Samarakis’ novel of the same name, which is a modern Greek classic and bestseller. It is also writer-director Theophilos Papastylianos’ (Algorhythm, Showtime) feature debut, and it is about to get its world premiere, appropriately in the Baltics. The film will debut in the Baltic Film Competition of the 30th edition of Estonia’s Tallinn Black Nights Film Festival (PÖFF) on Wednesday, Nov. 18. Related Stories Movies A Man Has Just Retired, But the Government Insists He Is Dead. Welcome to 'When I Existed'! (Exclusive Trailer, Plus Chat With the Directors) Lifestyle Soho House Creator Nick Jones Is Reinventing the Hotel -- Again

British talent leads The Flaw, with David Dawson, who plays Derek Jarman in Elsinore, portraying the Interrogator, and Arthur McBain (After the Flood, Napoleon) playing the Suspect. Also part of the ensemble cast are Ralf Little, Angela Brouskou, Lyne Renée, Sarah Akokhia and Georgia Small. Thodoris Zacharakis is the cinematographer, while Yannis Chalkiadakis is the editor. Produced by Panos Papahadzis and Papastylianos, the co-producers are Alise Rogule and Lina Yannopoulou. Jacques Kruger is the sales agent.

Papastylianos talked to THR ahead of the film’s premiere about just how timely and urgent The Flaw feels, its cast and visuals, and why the film is like one giant puzzle.

How did you approach adapting the novel after you read it?It was my second time reading the book, and it was a revelation for me because the perspective was completely different. I wasn’t reading it as a teenager, like the first time. Now, I was reading it as a director.

The most important part when I wrote the script was to take a step back and say, “Okay, this is an amazing idea, but the film is going to be released in five to seven years. Is it going to be amazing in seven years?”

That said, the film feels scarily timely.

It was instinct because I was seeing how the whole world was progressing and how society was changing. I felt this book was timeless, and as the years go by, it’s going to become more up to date all the time.

The weird thing for me is that, in a sense, I’m happy that the film is up to date. But on the other hand, I’m sad because that means our society and the world are taking a very dramatic shift towards a place that is not good.

Can you talk a bit about the themes of totalitarianism and beyond that The Flaw explores?

For me, the totalitarian regimes and everything is just the first level. I wanted the film to act as a strong allegory. You have the regimes, the governments, or society, that is all around us. But I actually think that each one of us is a regime by himself.

We perceive the world through our own eyes and our own perspective, and through our own prejudice. So, by definition, we are a regime by ourselves. Imagine a hunter walking in the woods, and all of a sudden, he sees a deer. So he lifts up his rifle and looks through the scope. At that moment, he sees prey. If he could just take a moment and lower the scope, he would see a deer again. And I think it’s the same thing with people.

We meet somebody, and we judge them right off the bat – their sexual orientation, [how they look], how political or non-political they are. During COVID, for example, there were two groups of people: the anti-vaccine and the vaccine people, but nothing in the middle. But for me, that’s the interesting thing: people meet in the middle.

The two protagonists in The Flaw go through an evolution, but I didn’t fully notice it until the end…

What we were aiming for was a slow change that the characters go through. We wanted to be so slow that you realize that the characters you’ve been seeing from the beginning, all of a sudden, at the end, are completely different characters.

The film has some spectacular-looking buildings. How did you think about locations?

We shot in Greece, and we also shot in Riga in Latvia. Riga was a very important part of the whole process in order to get that feeling of brutalism and all this dark, timeless sensation.

But then we had to combine Greece with Riga, and that was a challenge, so we could get a timeless space and locations where you don’t know they were actually shot in two different countries. The production design team did an amazing job. And lighting and framing were very important for us. I believe that when we shoot, we make conscious choices that actually affect people’s subconscious.

So every shot is there for a reason. Every close-up is there for a reason. We choose what we want to show or what we don’t want to show the audience.

The Flaw features Greece and Latvia as locations. But the two lead characters are played by British guys. Tell me a bit about the casting decisions.

It was amazing to work with all these guys. They are amazing professionals. I actually sent paintings, portraits, to the casting director. And I said, “Look, this is the energy that I’m looking for as a character.” I used a lot of [Amedeo] Modigliani in terms of the paintings that I sent, because he has this energy and these figures that are surreal, but at the same time, they’re actual people.

My process with the actors once they know the scenes is this. When they ask me what I think about a scene and the characters, I always say to them, “I don’t have anything to say to you. You know the script. You know the scenes. You know your character. Just present your approach.” You need to give actors space so they can breathe on their own.

Tell me a bit about Greek theater and film actress Angela Brouskou and her role of the “Head”?

What we were aiming at was that the Head should not be a natural person but should be on the edge of becoming an idea. She represents the Regime. She is the Regime. So you have a character, but the way she’s depicted, sometimes you think that she is a non-character. It’s like she’s becoming the idea of the regime. She becomes a persona that turns into an idea.

[Warning: This question and answer contains what may be considered a spoiler.]David and Arthur have such a fascinating dynamic and chemistry that keeps shifting. How did you approach their characters’ relationship?

For me there was a key thing. One is the alter ego of the other. So, our big challenge was to [make sure you] see hidden qualities that slowly start coming out more and more. And before the shoot, I showed them the paintings, the portraits, that [I had shared with the casting team]. And I also gave them two pieces of music they could listen to. I truly believe that our work has a lot to do with the energy of the people.

I am glad you mentioned music because that seems to play into the characters and story of The Flaw very strongly…

Yes, I got Dimitris Fessas, the composer, and we worked a lot with him. We wanted to have these rough-edged, train-like pieces. We wanted to use a lot of the electric guitar combined with violins or violas, and then he actually used a traditional Greek instrument called the Cretan lyra. There are actually scenes where the Cretan lyra is having this discussion with the electric guitar. And then a violin steps in, and the whole sensation is a little bit out of this world – in a good sense.

We created the melody of the Interrogator and the melody of the Suspect, but in the end, these two melodies have to interweave. The sound design was also an important [element of that]. We paid so much attention to every detail, especially sound-wise.

Talking to you makes me understand why I experienced The Flaw as pieces of a puzzle slowly emerging and a bigger picture coming into view.

I think that’s the best way to describe it. That’s amazing how you put it. Yeah, the whole film is pieces of a puzzle that have to come together.

China Box Office: ‘Traces of Justice’ Holds No. 1 Spot as ‘Probation’ and ‘Everest: The Other Side’ Enter Top Five

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

New Classics Pictures’ crime procedural drama “Traces of Justice” retained first place at the mainland China box office during the Nov. 13–15 weekend, grossing RMB22.6 million ($3.4 million) over the three-day frame, according to data from Artisan Gateway.

The steady seventh-weekend hold pushed the period investigation epic to a cumulative total of $109.6 million since its Oct. 1 release. Directed by Chen Sicheng (“Detective Chinatown”) and co-written with Zhang Ji and Yan Zijie, the drama stars Zhang Yi, Ma Li, and Chen Minghao alongside Karry Wang, Fan Chengcheng, and Chen Feiyu in a multi-decade narrative tracking an elite public security detective solving complex historical cases based on real events. Related Stories 'Adults' Renewed for Season 3 at FX

Constantin Film and Sony Pictures’ horror reboot “Resident Evil” held onto second position, taking in $2.7 million in its sixth weekend to lift its cumulative gross to $79.9 million since launching on Oct. 5.

Beijing Joy Fun Pictures’ domestic body-swap comedy “Mr. & Mrs. Pardon” was in third place, collecting $1.2 million over the three-day frame. Directed by Shen Chunyang and written by Ma Duo, the satirical feature stars real-life comedic pair Shen Chunyang and Xiao Shenyang alongside Jia Bing and Yu Yang, pushing its cumulative haul to $63.7 million since opening on Oct. 1.

iQIYI Pictures’ legal comedy-drama “Probation” debuted in fourth position, pulling in $945,000 over its two-day opening frame following a Nov. 14 release. Directed by Zong Haiying, the film stars Tong Liya as a single mother who receives a suspended sentence for involuntary manslaughter after stepping in as a Good Samaritan. While serving community probation, she teams up with three close friends and a lawyer- played by Wang Hao, Wang Zhi, and Wu You – to uncover the truth and clear her name.

National Geographic Documentary Films’ mountaineering documentary “Everest: The Other Side” rounded out the top five in its second weekend, earning $900,000 for a cumulative total of $1.9 million since its Nov. 6 arthouse rollout.

Mainland China’s overall weekend box office gross was at $15.3 million amid a prolonged fall lull. The 2026 year-to-date box office reached $4.78 billion, trailing the same period in 2025 by 29.5%.

Drake Maye limps to locker room, Tommy DeVito in at QB for Patriots in Germany

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Patriots quarterback Drake Maye suffered an injury on the last play of New England’s first offensive possession today in Germany.

On a third-and-goal, Lions defensive end Aidan Hutchinson took Maye down and appeared to land on his legs. Maye was slow to get up but walked off the field on his own, and there was initially no indication that he was seriously injured.

But at the start of the Patriots’ second drive, Maye limped to the locker room and backup Tommy DeVito came in to replace him. The Patriots said Maye is questionable to return with an ankle injury.

Patriots right tackle Morgan Moses has also left the game with an injury.

The Lions have an early 7-3 lead, but the injuries to two of the best players in the NFL may prove to matter more to both teams’ playoff hopes than the result of this game.

Resident Evil: Original Sin Announced For March 2027

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Resident Evil has had a pretty phenomenal year in 2026, with the critically acclaimed Resident Evil 9 proving to be a huge success among fans, and the Zach Cregger adaptation finally delivering an exceptional film within the RE universe. Naturally, Capcom has a lot of work to do if it wants 2027 and beyond to be just as compelling a year, and it looks like that may be possible, with a number of exciting new projects slated to be in the works.

Among all of the upcoming Resident Evil releases is Resident Evil: Original Sin - Prelude to Biohazard. This is targeted at diehard fans of the original game, offering a faithful recreation that looks to deliver the ultimate Resident Evil experience in a way most other adaptations have simply failed to do. Set to be released toward the start of the year, Original Sin could be the very Resident Evil project that fans have been clamoring for, and perhaps even usurp Cregger's acclaimed entry in the Resident Evil franchise.

Resident Evil: Original Sin - Prelude to Biohazard is a feature-length fan-made film that aims to tell the untold story set directly before the events of the original game. Featuring Chris Redfield, Jill Valentine, Rebecca Chambers, Albert Wesker, and the rest of the gang, Original Sin is attempting to be as faithful a recreation as it possibly can be. Made in collaboration with the Residence of Evil team (the same folks who made the Resident Evil fan films, The Keeper's Diary and Resurrection), Original Sin very much has the air of a project made by fans for the fans.

Resident Evil: Original Sin - Prelude to Biohazard is set "between the infamous Arklay Mountain murders and that fateful night when S.T.A.R.S. enters the Spencer Mansion."

It stars renowned cosplayers Michael "Cosblade" Young as Wesker, Annie Roig as Jill Valentine, and MimiDoesCosplay as Rebecca Chambers, as well as several other cast members who look the part in their faithfully recreated outfits. These cast members were showcased in a trailer for the film. Original Sin - Prelude to Biohazard is being directed by Brandon Salisbury, who has previously directed a documentary about the failed production of George A. Romero's Resident Evil movie.

Sailsbury explained that he believes "Hollywood may never give us an accurate video game movie adaptation of Resident Evil," something that may have been evidenced by Zach Cregger's more ambiguous adaptation that strays far from the established canon and characters in favor of a standalone experience within its universe. Resident Evil: Original Sin - Prelude to Biohazard was funded by fans via a successful IndieGoGo campaign that managed to garner $188,368 from 729 backers, and has already completed production, with the film now in its final stages.

Original Sin will be released for free via the Residence of Evil YouTube channel, as it is a non-profit production. However, if you're interested in picking up a Blu-ray or VHS copy of the film, you can still back it via its IndieGoGo campaign. The Blu-ray copy will cost you $65, while the VHS edition of the film is $200. It's exciting to be getting a new Resident Evil film, especially one that purports to be a more faithful adaptation, and hopefully it will quell fans' disappointment with Cregger's less direct approach.

Ridley Scott’s Blade Runner Franchise Officially Returns in 10 Days

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. He also writes obsessively about the box office, charting the many hits and misses that are released weekly, and how their commercial performance shapes public perception. In his time at Collider, he has also helped drive diversity by writing stories about the multiple Indian film industries, with a goal of introducing audiences to a whole new world of cinema.

Earlier this year, Ridley Scott expressed an interest in returning to the Alien franchise after nearly a decade. This would mean superseding the franchise's largely successful latest installment, Alien: Romulus, which Scott didn't direct. This wouldn't be the first time that the legendary filmmaker has scrubbed the existence of a sequel to one of his sci-fi classics. He serves as an executive producer on the upcoming Prime Video series Blade Runner 2099, a limited series reimagining of his neo-noir sci-fi universe that began in the 1980s.

The new series ignores the franchise's 2017 reboot, Blade Runner 2049. Incidentally, the film was released in the same year as Scott's Alien: Covenant, which received poorer reviews and earned about $40 million less at the worldwide box office. Both movies brought their respective franchises to a grinding halt, although Scott seems to be pleased enough with Covenant to continue its narrative.

He wasn't, however, a fan of Blade Runner 2049. The movie brought back the original film's star Harrison Ford, while Ryan Gosling played the new protagonist. Produced on a reported budget of around $180 million, the film grossed nearly $290 million worldwide and was deemed not successful enough to justify its high costs. Scott later said that he found the film to be too slow and too long, even though he claimed to have written most of it himself.

Directed by Denis Villeneuve, Blade Runner 2049 received tremendous reviews, with many calling it one of the best sequels of all time and perhaps even better than Scott's original. The movie holds a "Certified Fresh" 88% score on the aggregator website Rotten Tomatoes. Released in 1982, the original Blade Runner famously underperformed at the box office as well, grossing $42 million against a reported budget of $30 million. It holds a "Certified Fresh" 89% score on Rotten Tomatoes.

The new series, starring Michelle Yeoh and Hunter Schafer, has no such box-office pressures, although the streamer will certainly want it to hit certain viewership benchmarks. Prime Video has had an uneven run when it comes to mega-budget franchise titles, with The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power, Citadel, and The Terminal List struggling to retain viewers. Blade Runner 2099, created by Silka Luisa, will debut all eight episodes on November 25 and will likely overtake the recently released third season of The Rings of Power on the streaming charts. Stay tuned to Collider for more updates.

German fan favorite Amon-Ra St. Brown scores first TD in Munich

📅 2026-11-15  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Lions wide receiver Amon-Ra St. Brown has a German mother, speaks German and spent some of his childhood in Germany, so when the NFL sent the Lions to Munich, they knew he’d be a fan favorite. And it didn’t take St. Brown long to deliver.

On the Lions’ first possession, St. Brown caught a pass from Jared Goff, made the first tackler miss, cut back inside and raced to the end zone for a 56-yard touchdown.

The Lions completed two passes on the opening drive and St. Brown caught them both, for 63 yards.

Detroit has an early 7-3 lead over the Patriots.

Elon Musks shares POV footage of Starship after the catch

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Yesterday was a good day for SpaceX and Elon Musk, and a banner day in the history of man's quest for the stars.

Not only did the launch of Starship, the largest interplantery rocket in the SpaceX fleet, standing over 400 feet tall and weighing over 5,000 tons, go off without a hitch from the Starbase launchpad in Boca Chica, Texas, but the vessel successfully delivered its payload of 26 Starlink V3 satellites over the course of its 22.5-hour mission, according to reporting from CNN, while orbiting the Earth 14 times.

But the real sticking point was always going to be the landing, where the SpaceX team attempted to prove their "reusable" rocket theory by successfully catching the falling Starship with some well-timed rocket boosters and incredible feats of engineering. The catch has been attempted a few times before on prior missions, successfully for the Super Heavy Booster on three occasions, but yesterday marked the first time the actual Starship was caught by the company's giant mechanical arms.

Understandably elated, Musk took to X, the social media site formerly known as Twitter, to share his incredible POV from atop the tower, shortly after the Starship catch:

Because journalists and bystanders were relegated to a safe watch site far away from the Starship catch site, most of the photos and footage of the catch struggles to convey the sheer size of the spaceship, but Musk's perspective gives a better sense of the scale of the ship and, therefore, the incredible challenge of executing a soft landing.

And for a better sense of how incredible the maneuver itself was, check out this time-lapse shot also shared by Musk:

If you missed the high drama of this most recent launch and landing, no worries: the next SpaceX mission, USSF-317, is around the corner.

Sydney Sweeney's Madame Web Is Now Streaming On HBO Max

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Sydney Sweeney is back on streaming with her controversial superhero movie based on Marvel characters, which is now available on HBO Max. Sweeney's list of movies and TV shows is filled with hits, and the actress has a huge relationship with HBO. After all, it was at the network that she got her big break, with Euphoria making Sweeney a rising star.

After that, she went on to play a key role in the first season of what has become one of the best HBO TV shows of all time, the dark comedy series The White Lotus. On the big screen, the star has started to play around with genre fare, and Sweeney's list of upcoming movies is filled with exciting sci-fi projects and more.

Among those films is the video game adaptation Split Fiction, which mixes sci-fi and fantasy, and the Netflix Gundam movie with Noah Centineo, which will see Sweeney play a mech pilot in what should be one of her biggest projects to date. However, before both of those movies, the actress came to join a tangential Marvel universe.

Sweeney is one of the major stars who ended up becoming part of Sony's Spider-Man Universe, which has that name because it focused on villains and supporting characters from Marvel Comics tied to Spider-Man, not the hero himself. Sweeney starred in Madame Web, and the film has now swung onto a major streaming service, allowing Marvel fans to experience it again.

Madame Web arrived on HBO Max this Saturday, November 14, and despite it not having been a theatrical hit, the superhero movie is expected to rise quickly on the streamer's nationwide chart, repeating the huge success the film saw on Netflix a couple of years ago. Madame Web is part of the new arrivals on HBO Max in November, with movies like Elf, The First Purge, Happy Feet, Jerry Maguire, Jurassic World: Fallen Kingdom, The Polar Express, Scream, Sherlock Holmes: A Game of Shadows, multiple Planet of the Apes films, and even fellow Marvel movie The Wolverine also joining the streaming service.

The best movies to watch on HBO Max this weekend are Sydney Sweeney's sci-fi thriller, a dystopian sci-fi, and a violent horror franchise entry.

Madame Web follows Dakota Johnson's Cassie Webb, a paramedic who tries to understand her clairvoyant powers, being able to change the future she sees to keep people safe. Sweeney plays Julia Cornwall, one of the three teenagers destined to become Spider-Women and kill the villain Ezekiel Sims, who wears a Spider-Man-like suit. The movie's major Spider-Man connection comes from the fact that Peter Parker is in his mother's belly in Madame Web, with Adam Scott also playing a young version of Uncle Ben, who is Cassie's partner and best friend. Madame Web had a lot of exciting factors, but it just did not work out.

Madame Web sits at a disappointing 10% critics' score on Rotten Tomatoes based on 270 reviews, earning the infamous Rotten designation on the review aggregator. While it is better, Madame Web's 53% audience score is still far from enticing. The universally negative reception to the film led to Sweeney's Marvel movie becoming a box office bomb, as Madame Web only grossed $100.5 million worldwide against a reported budget of $80 million. When accounting for marketing and other expenses, it needed to finish between $160 million and $200 million to break even, which did not happen.

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Madame Web's overall failure comes down to a wide array of factors, chief among them being the fact that the film's script was simply lackluster, with unnatural and convoluted dialogue and an uneven story. On top of that, multiple actors delivered wooden performances, with the most egregious case being how the film used ADR for a lot of its villain's dialogue, and so Ezekiel Sims became an unbearable character to watch, looking like he was dubbed over every time he spoke.

There is also the fact that the Spider-Women suits and powers only appear in a vision at the very end of the movie, teasing the film many paid to see when buying tickets for Madame Web. As such, what the Sony Marvel movie actually ended up being was a convoluted mess, though its star power and the fact that its flaws make it an unintentional comedy should help Madame Web find streaming success on HBO Max years after its theatrical release.

Aidan Hutchinson injured celebrating after sacking Drake Maye in Germany

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Lions defensive end Aidan Hutchinson was injured celebrating after sacking Drake Maye on third-and-goal at the end of the Patriots’ first possession early Sunday in Munich, Germany.

Maye appeared to be banged up on the sack as well, but Hutchinson got up, jumped in the air celebrating, and then immediately seemed to feel something wrong as he landed. He fell to the ground and grabbed at his right lower leg.

Hutchinson was able to walk off the field with a limp. After getting examined on the sideline for a few minutes, he walked to the locker room with the Lions’ medical staff. There was no immediate word on the nature of his injury.

The Patriots kicked a field goal to take a 3-0 lead.

Blade Runner 2099 bosses hint at a possible "triple-A" Blade Runner game in the future - just don't expect it any time soon

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Blade Runner 2099 - an upcoming TV miniseries coming to Amazon Prime Video, acting as a sequel to Blade Runner (1982) and Blade Runner 2049 (2017) - might be getting a video game. And a 'triple-A' video game, at that. At least that's according to Andrew Kosove and Broderick Johnson, executive producers for Blade Runner 2099 and co-CEOs of Alcon Entertainment (which owns the Blade Runner IP).

In a new interview with IGN, the duo reveal that they had thought about adapting the Blade Runner fracnhise into a game, and that - should it come to fruition - it would target "triple-A" in scope. Other than that, the duo seemed to want to keep any potential information to themselves. Given they appear to be in talks about the title, rather than in any active development, if something did come of this ideation, it would no doubt be a long, long way off. Kosove and Johnson's aim seems to be getting players to "interact" more with the world of Blade Runner, which has recently been fleshed out by the 2017 movie and is set to be developed more in the upcoming series.

Of course, we've seen Blade Runner games before. There was 1997's simply-titled Blade Runner - developed by Westwood Studios and published by Virgin Interactive - which was a point-and-click adventure that really seemed to pick up the vibes the original Ridley Scott was putting down. An Enhanced Edition of the game was released in 2022, which is actually more of a remake than a simple enhancement.

Then, in 2023, Annapurna Interactive unveiled its very first internally-developed game, in the form of Blade Runner 2033: Labyrinth. The game, which promised to riff on the dystopian universe established in Ridley Scott's cult classic movie, would have been sandwiched squarely between that film and its sequel, Blade Runner 2049. It was going to be set in LA after the Blackout hits, but beyond that - and the fact it's coming to PC and consoles at some point - few details ever managed to break through the PR barrier before the game went dark: it's been removed from Annapurna's website and delisted from Steam. Curious.

If you want to see something more from the cyberpunk world sooner, Blade Runner 2099 releases on Prime Video on 25th November.

Richard Sterban, Oak Ridge Boys’ Bass Singer, Dies at 83

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Sterban died Nov. 14 in Hendersonville, Tennessee, due to complications from bronchitis and pneumonia after fighting a battle with cancer, his family and the music group announced.

Richard Sterban, the Oak Ridge Boys’ bass singer who memorably lent his vocals to their beloved “Elvira” cover, has died. He was 83.

Sterban died Nov. 14 in Hendersonville, Tennessee, due to complications from bronchitis and pneumonia after fighting a battle with cancer, his family and the Oak Ridge Boys announced.

“We appreciate all of your love, support, and prayers. Please continue to lift us up in this difficult time, we find comfort knowing that he is currently face to face with Jesus,” Sterban’s family said in a statement. Related Stories TV Dawn Greenhalgh, Voice of the Elephant Queen Celeste in 'Babar' Cartoons, Dies at 93 Music Gerry Griffith, Music Executive Who Discovered Whitney Houston, Dies at 87

Born April 24, 1943, in Camden, New Jersey, Sterban began singing at six in Sunday school as a soprano. He sang tenor in his seventh-grade glee club, and upon his eighth grade, his voice dropped to bass.

He studied music at Trenton State College (modernly known as the College of New Jersey), and began singing in a gospel quartet where he met Joe Bonsall, his future Oak Ridge Boys bandmate.

While singing part-time, Sterban joined J.D. Sumner and the Stamps, whom Elvis Presley hired to sing behind him. He then sang and recorded with the King of Rock and Roll, and also appeared in the 1972 concert film Elvis on Tour.

Sterban was then offered the opportunity to join the Oak Ridge Boys after the departure of Noel Fox, which he accepted in 1972. Bonsall joined the group one year later, with Duane Allen, William Lee Golden and Sterban already in the group, which carried on as the faces of the band for five decades.

They performed with Johnny Cash, who helped them sign with Columbia Records in 1973, though it was after opening for Roy Clark in 1975 when they met his manger, Jim Halsey, who would later sign with the group.

They reached success with “Y’all Come Back Saloon,” which reached No. 3 on the country chart in 1977, and led to a string of hits. “I’ll Be True to You” became their first No. 1 song, though 1981’s “Elvira” would become one of their largest successes.

Originally recorded by Dallas Frazier, the Oak Ridge Boys’ version of the track went to No. 1 on the country chart and No. 5 on the pop chart, sold more than two million copies and won a Grammy. Sterban’s bass refrain was a major touchpoint on the song.

The Oak Ridge Boys went on to also become members of the Grand Ole Opry, the Gospel Music Hall of Fame and the Country Music Hall of Fame.

Sterban released his memoir From Elvis to Elvira: My Life on Stage, written with Steven Robinson, in 2012.

Sterban is survived by his wife, Donna; his daughters, Lauren (Tyler) and Tori (Michael); his sons, Rich (Chele), Doug, and Chris; his grandchildren, Mali (Zachary), Nikolas, Tyler (Ryann), Madelyn, Alexandra, Rex, Owen, and Charli Grace; and his great-grandchildren, Salem, Asher, and Theo. He was preceded in death by his mother Victoria Sterban, brother Joseph Sterban and grandson Zachary.

Panthers-Saints game won't have a replay assistant, due to ground stop

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The nationwide ground stop that shut down the aviation system has impacted the NFL’s officiating assignments for Sunday games. And while the league has announced the specific changes that will result in non-referees serving as referees for four games on Sunday (with side judge Don Willard working two games in the same day), other adjustments have not been officially announced.

There surely will be others. For now, one game will be shorthanded.

Via Tom Pelissero of Netflix, the Panthers-Saints game won’t have a replay assistant.

Those duties will be handled by the league office. Which will put a strain on the usual resources there, especially in the cluster of 1:00 p.m. ET games. (Panthers-Saints is one of the seven games to be played in that window.)

Given that the league has otherwise adjusted the Week 10 assignments to keep the officials within driving distance of their hometowns, it’s likely that the existing crews will be altered significantly. Which will introduce unfamiliarity and otherwise complicate the effort to get calls right on a consistent basis.

Absent an advance announcement from the league as to the full extent of the changes, they won’t be known until the NFL posts the official Game Books for the Sunday contests.

John Wick Faces Off Against Ballerina in This Invasion Thriller Officially Leaving HBO Max

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. He also writes obsessively about the box office, charting the many hits and misses that are released weekly, and how their commercial performance shapes public perception. In his time at Collider, he has also helped drive diversity by writing stories about the multiple Indian film industries, with a goal of introducing audiences to a whole new world of cinema.

Years before they shared the screen in From the World of John Wick: Ballerina, Ana de Armas and Keanu Reeves starred together in an erotic exploitation movie directed by Eli Roth. A friend and collaborator of Quentin Tarantino, Roth had already established his genre credentials with the horror film Hostel and the cannibal survival thriller The Green Inferno — both are regarded as some of the most transgressive movies made in mainstream Hollywood. Roth's home invasion thriller with De Armas and Reeves wasn't as edgy, although it was still shocking enough to remain in its niche. The film in question is currently streaming on HBO Max in the United States, giving fans of genre cinema a great opportunity to check it out.

It won't, however, be available for much longer. The movie was released a year after the first John Wick, which emerged as a surprise hit and reinvigorated Reeves' career as an action star. The rest is history, with the John Wick franchise having generated more than $1 billion in global box-office revenue in the decade since. Reeves is set to return as the titular assassin in a fifth film, following his extended cameo in Ballerina, which was released last year.

Ballerina marked De Armas' first time leading a potential franchise-starter of her own, after having delivered memorable performances in hits such as Knives Out, No Time to Die, and the Oscar-nominated Blonde. Despite mostly positive reviews, Ballerina underperformed at the box office, grossing around $140 million against a reported budget of $90 million. De Armas wasn't a household name back in 2015, when she first worked with Reeves in the home invasion thriller.

Four franchises, four completely different ways of getting underneath your skin. This is the one built out of your particular fear, why it works so well on you, and what it would leave behind.

Your nightmare does not wait outside the door. It waits for you to close your eyes, which you are going to have to do eventually, because nobody has ever won that particular argument.

Your nightmare is not really the doll. It is the face of the person you tell, and the way they decide, very kindly, that you have imagined all of it.

Your nightmare has your number, knows your name, and has almost certainly been inside your house before. The worst part is that you already know them.

Your nightmare is not really about you. It is about the people asleep down the hall, and the feeling that whatever is here is not interested in leaving.

We're talking, of course, about Knock Knock. The movie follows two young women who show up at the doorstep of an architect (Reeves) and coerce him into committing a sexual transgression. Soon, the architect finds himself at their mercy, as they hold him hostage in his house. The movie received mixed reviews and is now sitting at a 37% score on Rotten Tomatoes, where the consensus reads, "Knock Knock brings a lot of talent to bear on its satirical approach to torture horror, but not effectively enough to overcome its repetitive story or misguidedly campy tone."

Audiences were apparently not on the same page either, awarding it a poor 19% score on the aggregator website. Knock Knock grossed around $6 million at the box office against a reported budget of $10 million. Reeves will next star in another survival thriller — Shiver, directed by Deadpool filmmaker Tim Miller. You can watch Knock Knock on HBO Max until December 1, and stay tuned to Collider for more updates.

A devoted husband and father helps two stranded young women who knock on his door, but his kind gesture turns into a dangerous seduction and a deadly game of cat and mouse.

Meryl Streep, Jon Hamm, Dave Franco Brought Comedy To ‘Hoppers’, Director Daniel Chong Says – Contenders L.A.

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Pixar animated film Hoppers features an eclectic voice cast, from Meryl Streep and Jon Hamm to Dave Franco and Bobby Moynihan. Whatever their background, director Daniel Chong wanted funny people. At Contenders Film, Chong said comedy drove the casting.

“Comedy was the No. 1 thing we wanted as casting,” Chong said. “[Hamm’s] post-Mad Men career has been a lot of comedy. That guy is not afraid to look stupid. We definitely put him through it in the movie. Meryl was also down for jokes. She came in with that really weird voice. She came in hot with that voice and we were like okay, here we go.”

Hamm plays the mayor who is approving the razing of a glade. Meryl Streep plays the insect queen. In the film, young Mabel (Piper Curda), uses Hopper technology to control a robot beaver. As a beaver, she rallies the animals to save the glade. Watch on Deadline

Franco plays Streep’s son and worked hard to keep up.

“With Dave, we also put him through the ringer because that is a very high octane performance,” Chong said. “He has to go to 11 every line. He was sweating after every take.”

Curda joined Chong and producer Nicole Paradis Grindle at Contenders. Curda was a TikToker, Chong discovered.

“That’s not why I made TikTok,” Curda said. “I was bored. Now I’m here.”

Mabel is a young environmentalist. Curda hopes her passion inspires young viewers.

“What’s so cool about Mabel is she’s not shy about really caring and also at the same time, she’s incredibly cool,” Curda said. “it’s such a cool takeaway, especially for a young person, to be like it’s cool to care. You can care a lot about something maybe most people don’t care about and still be awesome. I think it’s really cool to care.”

Grindle acknowledged there is a message to Hoppers. Comedy helps them deliver it, as she quoted another Disney classic.

“A spoonful of sugar makes the medicine go down,” Grindle said. “We’re all about the sugar. It’s more meaningful when it’s rooted in something deeper. You need to have that kind of emotional catharsis to come out of the movie.”

For Week 9, Jalen Pitre was fined twice for illegal use of helmet

📅 2026-11-15  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Every Saturday, plenty of players get fined for illegal use of helmet fouls from the prior weekend’s game. For Week 9, Texans safety Jalen Pitre got fined twice.

Both instances occurred in the second quarter of the victory over the Chargers. Pitre was penalized for neither infraction.

Each fine will cost $15,400, subject to appeal. They were Pitre’s first fines of the season.

For the season (including the preseason), 53 fines have been imposed for illegal use of the helmet.

The NFL will impose larger fines on Pitre for future violations. Eventually, he’ll be at risk for a suspension.

Use of the helmet isn’t one of the five safety rules that trigger a one-game suspension in the event of three violations.
